We will then make the following standard assumption in this setting:

\displaystyle f_{\beta}=-\Phi\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right)\right)\overset{!}{=}-\text{crit}_{t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}}\Phi\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)\,,

(D.51)

where we introduced

\displaystyle\Phi\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right):=\left(\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}\right|_{R=0}\Phi^{(R)}\right)\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)\,.

(D.52)

This assumption is convenient because it reduces the calculation of the free energy density to the study of the critical points of
\Phi
, i.e., directly in the limit
R\downarrow 0
, instead of
\Phi^{(R)}
, thus simplifying subsequent calculations. We calculate the necessary expressions to get
\Phi
in our problem in Sections
D.9
and
D.10
. Note that for unique critical points, equation (
D.51
) is equivalent to saying that the limit as
R\downarrow 0
of the critical point
\left(t^{*}(R),\hat{t}^{*}(R)\right)
of the function
\Phi^{(R)}
converges to the critical point of the function
\Phi
. This result is not
a priori
obvious since, by differentiating the optimality condition for
\Phi^{(R)}
in (
D.46
) with respect to
R
and letting
R\downarrow 0
, we find

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\nabla_{t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\Phi^{(R)}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R)\right)\\
\nabla_{\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}}\Phi^{(R)}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R)\right)\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
0\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\nabla_{t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\Phi\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right)\right)\\
\nabla_{\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}}\Phi\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right)\right)\end{array}\right)+\nabla^{2}\Phi^{\left(R\downarrow 0\right)}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right)\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\mathrm{d}t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R)}{\mathrm{d}R}\\
\frac{\mathrm{d}\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R)}{\mathrm{d}R}\end{array}\right)_{R\downarrow 0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
0\end{array}\right)\,,

which means that for (
D.51
) to hold, the second term above, i.e., the Hessian of
\Phi^{(R)}
applied to the derivative of the critical point with respect to
R
, has to vanish in the limit
R\downarrow 0
. In general, it is easy to construct counter-examples where, for instance, (
D.51
) does not hold. One such case: suppose

\Phi^{(R)}(t_{\mathrm{sym}})=\frac{1}{2}(t_{\mathrm{sym}}-R)^{2}

for
t_{\mathrm{sym}}\in\mathbb{R}
, then
t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R)=R
, hence
\Phi^{(R)}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R)\right)\equiv 0
for any
R
, so the consistency condition (
D.45
) holds trivially,
\Phi(t_{\mathrm{sym}})=-t_{\mathrm{sym}}
. The left-hand side in (
D.51
) evaluates to
0
, but the right-hand side is not defined as
\Phi
does not have any critical points. Nevertheless, we will just assume that (
D.51
) holds for our particular setting following standard practice.

D.6
Simplifying
\Psi_{y}^{(R)}
for the replica-symmetric ansatz

Recalling the definition

\Upsilon^{(1:R)}=\left(\Upsilon^{(1)},\ldots,\Upsilon^{(R)}\right)=\left(y^{(1)},\,(V_{k}^{\top}y^{\prime})^{(1)},\,\ldots,\,y^{(R)},\,(V_{k}^{\top}y^{\prime})^{(R)}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)R}

of the replicated network outputs for convenience, here we transform the potential

\displaystyle\Psi^{(R)}_{y}(t)

\displaystyle=\alpha\log\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}\nu(\omega\,|\,t)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)R}}\mathrm{d}\nu(\Upsilon^{(1:R)}\,|\,\omega,t)L^{\Pi}_{\beta}\left(\Upsilon,\Upsilon^{(1:R)}\right)P_{\text{data}}\left(\Upsilon\,|\,\omega,\varpi\right)\right)

(D.63)

as defined in (
D.37
) within the replica-symmetric ansatz so that
R
becomes a parameter that can take on non-integer values. We will simplify the innermost expectation with respect to the Gaussian variables
\Upsilon^{(1:R)}\,|\,\omega,t
in particular. Regrouping the replica-symmetric overlap parameters into

\displaystyle t_{\mathrm{sym}}:=

\displaystyle\left(\rho_{a},\ s=\begin{pmatrix}s_{a}\\
s_{b}\end{pmatrix},\ f=\begin{pmatrix}f_{a}\\
f_{b}\end{pmatrix},\ q=\begin{pmatrix}q_{a}&q_{b}^{\top}\\
q_{b}&q_{c}\end{pmatrix},\ r=\begin{pmatrix}r_{a}&r_{b}^{\top}\\
r_{b}&r_{c}\end{pmatrix}\right)\,,

(D.64)

we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\nu\left(\Upsilon^{(1:R)}\,|\,\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-\frac{R(k+1)}{2}}\left(\det\Sigma^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)^{-1/2}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\left(\Upsilon^{(1:R)}-\mu^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)^{\top}\left(\Sigma^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)^{-1}\left(\Upsilon^{(1:R)}-\mu^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)\right\}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon^{(1:R)}\,,

(D.65)

with conditional mean and covariance

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mu^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}=\textsc{vec}\left(\1_{R}\otimes\left(s+\frac{\omega}{\rho_{a}}f\right)\right)\,,\\
\Sigma^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}=\1_{R}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\left(q-\frac{1}{\rho_{a}}f^{\otimes 2}\right)+I_{R}\otimes(r-q)\,.\end{cases}

(D.66)

Expanding the quadratic form in the exponent, we have

\displaystyle\biggl\langle\Upsilon^{(1:R)}-\mu^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\,\left(\Sigma^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}})}\right)^{-1}\left(\Upsilon^{(1:R)}-\mu^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)\biggr\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{r^{\prime}\in[R]}\sum_{r^{\prime\prime}\in[R]}\left\langle\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\widetilde{q}\left(\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)\right\rangle+\sum_{r^{\prime}\in[R]}\left\langle\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\left(\widetilde{r}-\widetilde{q}\right)\left(\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)\right\rangle.

(D.67)

where we write

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=:\1_{R}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\,\widetilde{q}+I_{R}\otimes\,\left(\widetilde{r}-\widetilde{q}\right)\,,

(D.68)

to separate the off-diagonal and diagonal terms of the inverse conditional covariance matrix.
We then replace the double-summation over
r
with a single summation using a multi-dimensional Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation:

\displaystyle\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{r^{\prime}\in[R]}\sum_{r^{\prime\prime}\in[R]}\left\langle\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\widetilde{q}\left(\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)\right\rangle\right\}=\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\prod_{r^{\prime}\in[R]}\exp\left\{\left\langle\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\sqrt{-\widetilde{q}}\;\xi\right\rangle\right\}\right].

(D.69)

Altogether, these results let us express the conditional Gaussian density of
\Upsilon^{(1:R)}\mid\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\nu\left(\Upsilon^{(1:R)}\mid\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-\frac{R(k+1)}{2}}\left(\det\Sigma^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\prod_{r^{\prime}\in[R]}\exp\left\{\left\langle\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\sqrt{-\widetilde{q}}\;\xi\right\rangle\right\}\right]\times

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{r^{\prime}\in[R]}\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\left(\widetilde{r}-\widetilde{q}\right)\left(\Upsilon^{(r^{\prime})}-\mu^{(r^{\prime})}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)\right\rangle\right\}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon^{(1:R)}\,,

(D.70)

resulting in

\displaystyle\ \exp\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha}\Psi_{y}^{(R)}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)\right\}=(2\pi)^{-\frac{R(k+1)}{2}}\left(\det\Sigma^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,I_{k+1}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\rho_{a})}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;P_{\text{data}}(\Upsilon\mid\omega,\varpi)\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Upsilon}\;L_{\beta}(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon})\exp\left\{\left\langle\tilde{\Upsilon}-\mu_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\sqrt{-\widetilde{q}}\;\xi\right\rangle\right\}\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\tilde{\Upsilon}-\mu_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\left(\widetilde{r}-\widetilde{q}\right)\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}-\mu_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)\right\rangle\right\}\Bigg)^{R}\Bigg]\,.

(D.71)

The integration variable
\tilde{\Upsilon}
denotes any of the
R
decoupled replicas. Expressing the potential
\Psi_{y}^{(R)}
in this way will now allow us to take non-integer
R
in the subsequent sections. Of course, a similar expression holds for
\Psi^{(R)}_{y_{0}}
upon replacing
L_{\beta}
with
\tilde{L}_{\beta h}
.

D.7
Simplifying
\Psi_{w}^{(R)}
for the replica-symmetric ansatz

Next, we simplify the second potential
\Psi_{w}^{(R)}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)
in (
D.36
) so that
R
may take on non-integer values. The potential is defined as

\displaystyle\Psi^{(R)}_{w}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\log\left(\left(\beta\lambda\right)^{-pR/2}\mathbb{E}_{\mid\varpi}\left[e^{\langle\hat{T}_{\mathrm{sym}},T(\theta_{0},\Theta,V_{k},W)\rangle}\right]\right)

(D.72)

within the replica-symmetric ansatz, where we understand the inner product notation to mean

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{T}_{\mathrm{sym}},T(\theta_{0},\Theta,V_{k},W)\right\rangle:=\left\langle\hat{\mu}_{\mathrm{sym}},\,\mu(\theta_{0},\Theta,V_{k},W)\right\rangle+\left\langle\textsc{vech}(\hat{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{sym}}),\;\textsc{vech}(\Sigma(\theta_{0},\Theta,V_{k},W))\right\rangle

\displaystyle~=d\hat{\rho}_{a}\|\theta_{0}\|^{2}+\sqrt{p}\hat{s}_{a}\sum_{r\in[R]}\kappa_{0}\left\langle w^{(r)},\1_{p}\right\rangle+\sqrt{p}\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{s}_{b},\kappa_{0}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta w^{(r)}\right\rangle+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\hat{f}_{a}\kappa_{1}\left\langle\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0},w^{(r)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{f}_{b},\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\right\rangle+p\sum_{r\neq r^{\prime}\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{q}_{b},\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}\Theta w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{r}_{b},\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}\Theta w^{(r)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r^{\prime}\in[R],\\
r^{\prime}>r\end{subarray}}\hat{q}_{a}\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\left\langle\Theta w^{(r)},\Theta w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle+\kappa_{*}^{2}\left\langle w^{(r)},w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle\right)+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\hat{r}_{a}\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\left\langle\Theta w^{(r)},\Theta w^{(r)}\right\rangle+\kappa_{*}^{2}\left\langle w^{(r)},w^{(r)}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle~+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r^{\prime}\in[R],\\
r^{\prime}>r\end{subarray}}\left\langle\hat{q}_{c},V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r^{\prime})}\right)\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle~+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{r}_{c},V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\right\rangle_{\text{HF}}\;.

(D.73)

Note that additional care must be taken not to double count the symmetric entries for the matrices along the block diagonal. To this purpose, above we use the “half Frobenius inner product”
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{HF}}
, which is related to the traditional Frobenius inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{F}}
with
\langle A,B\rangle_{\text{F}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{k}A_{ij}B_{ij}
for
A,B\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
via

2\langle P,Q\rangle_{\text{HF}}=\langle P,Q\rangle_{\text{F}}+\langle P,Q\odot I_{k}\rangle_{\text{F}}\,,\text{ for }P=P^{\top},\;Q=Q^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}\,,

(D.74)

where
\odot
denotes the Hadamard product with
(A\odot B)_{ij}=A_{ij}B_{ij}
.
Using this definition, as well as the analogous scalar-valued identity

\sum_{j}\sum_{i<j}s_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j}\sum_{i\neq j}s_{ij}
for symmetric
s
, we express the above as

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{T}_{\mathrm{sym}},T(\theta_{0},\Theta,V_{k},W)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=d\hat{\rho}_{a}\|\theta_{0}\|^{2}+\sqrt{p}\hat{s}_{a}\sum_{r\in[R]}\kappa_{0}\left\langle w^{(r)},\1_{p}\right\rangle+\sqrt{p}\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{s}_{b},\kappa_{0}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta w^{(r)}\right\rangle+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\hat{f}_{a}\kappa_{1}\left\langle\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0},w^{(r)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{f}_{b},\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\right\rangle+p\sum_{r,r^{\prime}\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{q}_{b},\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}\Theta w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{r}_{b}-\hat{q}_{b},\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}\Theta w^{(r)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r,r^{\prime}\in[R]}\hat{q}_{a}\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\left\langle\Theta w^{(r)},\Theta w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle+\kappa_{*}^{2}\left\langle w^{(r)},w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle\right)+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left(\hat{r}_{a}-\frac{1}{2}\hat{q}_{a}\right)\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\left\langle\Theta w^{(r)},\Theta w^{(r)}\right\rangle+\kappa_{*}^{2}\left\langle w^{(r)},w^{(r)}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r,r^{\prime}\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{q}_{c},V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r^{\prime})}\right)\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{r}_{c}\odot I_{k},V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\hat{r}_{c}-\hat{q}_{c},V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\;\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)\textsc{diag}\left(w^{(r)}\right)\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}.

(D.75)

We make the
w
-dependencies clear by re-arranging the terms above to yield

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{T}_{\mathrm{sym}},T(\theta_{0},\Theta,V_{k},W)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=d\hat{\rho}_{a}\|\theta_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\sqrt{p}\hat{s}_{a}\sum_{r\in[R]}\kappa_{0}\left\langle\1_{p},w^{(r)}\right\rangle+\sqrt{p}\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{s}_{b},w^{(r)}\right\rangle+p\sum_{r\in[R]}\hat{f}_{a}\kappa_{1}\left\langle\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0},w^{(r)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+p\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b})\odot(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}),w^{(r)}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r,r^{\prime}\in[R]}\left\langle w^{(r)},2\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}\left(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{q}_{b}\right)w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle w^{(r)},2\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}(\hat{r}_{b}-\hat{q}_{b}))w^{(r)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r,r^{\prime}\in[R]}\hat{q}_{a1}\left\langle w^{(r)},\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+\kappa_{*}^{2}I_{p}\right)w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left(2\hat{r}_{a}-\hat{q}_{a}\right)\left\langle w^{(r)},\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+\kappa_{*}^{2}I_{p}\right)w^{(r)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r,r^{\prime}\in[R]}\left\langle w^{(r)},\left(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{q}_{c}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\right)\odot\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)w^{(r^{\prime})}\right\rangle

\displaystyle~+\frac{1}{2}p\sum_{r\in[R]}\left\langle w^{(r)},\left(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\left(\hat{r}_{c}\odot I_{k}+\hat{r}_{c}-\hat{q}_{c}\right)V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\right)\odot\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)w^{(r)}\right\rangle\;.

(D.76)

To shorten the notation, we introduce the auxiliary definitions

\displaystyle\begin{cases}J_{\hat{s}_{a}}&=\kappa_{0}\hat{s}_{a}\1_{p}\\
J_{\hat{s}_{b}}&=\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{s}_{b}\\
J_{\hat{f}_{a}}&=\sqrt{p}\hat{f}_{a}\kappa_{1}\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\\
J_{\hat{f}_{b}}&=\sqrt{p}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b})\odot(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0})\end{cases}

(D.77)

and

\displaystyle\begin{cases}A_{\hat{q}_{b}}&=2\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}\left(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{q}_{b}\right)\\
A_{\hat{r}_{b}-\hat{q}_{b}}&=-2\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}\left(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}(\hat{r}_{b}-\hat{q}_{b})\right)\\
A_{\hat{q}_{a}}&=\hat{q}_{a}\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+\kappa_{*}^{2}I_{p}\right)\\
A_{2\hat{r}_{a}-\hat{q}_{a}}&=-\left(2\hat{r}_{a}-\hat{q}_{a}\right)\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+\kappa_{*}^{2}I_{p}\right)\\
A_{\hat{q}_{c}}&=\left(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{q}_{c}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\right)\odot\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)\\
A_{2\hat{r}_{c}-\hat{q}_{c}}&=-\left(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\left(\hat{r}_{c}\odot I_{k}+\hat{r}_{c}-\hat{q}_{c}\right)V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\right)\odot\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)\\
&=-\left(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\left(\hat{r}_{c}\odot\left(I_{k}+\1_{k}^{\otimes 2}\right)-\hat{q}_{c}\right)V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\right)\odot\left((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p}\right)\,.\end{cases}

(D.78)

In order to decouple the replicas, we again make use of Hubbard–Stratonovich transformations for all terms that involve double summations. This yields

\displaystyle\exp\left\{\left\langle\hat{T}_{\mathrm{sym}},T(\theta_{0},\Theta,V_{k},W)\right\rangle\right\}=\exp\left\{d\hat{\rho}_{a}\left\lVert\theta_{0}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{\hat{q}_{b}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{a}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{c}}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,I_{p}\right)}\bigg[\prod_{r\in[R]}\bigg(\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}p\left\langle w^{(r)},\left[A_{\hat{r}_{b}-\hat{q}_{b}}+A_{2\hat{r}_{a}-\hat{q}_{a}}+A_{2\hat{r}_{c}-\hat{q}_{c}}\right]w^{(r)}\right\rangle+\sqrt{p}\left\langle w^{(r)},J\right\rangle\right\}\bigg)\bigg]

(D.79)

with “source” term
J:=J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}}+J_{\hat{f}_{a}}+J_{\hat{f}_{b}}+A_{\hat{q}_{b}}^{1/2}\eta_{\hat{q}_{b}}+A_{\hat{q}_{a}}^{1/2}\eta_{\hat{q}_{a}}+A_{\hat{q}_{c}}^{1/2}\eta_{\hat{q}_{c}}
.
All in all, we then obtain

\displaystyle\exp\left\{p\Psi_{w}^{(R)}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)\right\}=\exp\left\{d\hat{\rho}_{a}\left\lVert\theta_{0}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}\times

\displaystyle\;\;\times\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{\hat{q}_{b1}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{a1}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{c1}}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,I_{p}\right)}\bigg[\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{w}\bigg[\left(\beta\lambda\right)^{-p/2}\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}p\left\langle w,\left[A_{\hat{r}_{b}-\hat{q}_{b}}+A_{2\hat{r}_{a}-\hat{q}_{a}}+A_{2\hat{r}_{c}-\hat{q}_{c}}\right]w\right\rangle+\sqrt{p}\left\langle J,w\right\rangle\right\}\bigg]\bigg)^{R}\bigg]\,,

(D.80)

such that again the
R
-dependence is explicit and allows for taking non-integer values of
R
.

D.8
Consistency check for the replica-symmetric ansatz and determining
\rho_{a},\hat{\rho}_{a}

We conclude from the results (
D.71
) and (
D.80
) of the calculations of the previous two sections, as well as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\langle t,\hat{t}\rangle=

\displaystyle\gamma\rho_{a}\hat{\rho}_{a}+\frac{R}{\sqrt{p}}s_{a}\hat{s}_{a}+\frac{R}{\sqrt{p}}\left\langle s_{b},\hat{s}_{b}\right\rangle+Rf_{a}\hat{f}_{a}+R\left\langle f_{b},\hat{f}_{b}\right\rangle+\frac{R(R-1)}{2}q_{a}\hat{q}_{a}

\displaystyle+Rr_{a}\hat{r}_{a}+R(R-1)\left\langle q_{b},\hat{q}_{b}\right\rangle+R\left\langle r_{b},\hat{r}_{b}\right\rangle+\frac{R(R-1)}{2}\langle q_{c},\hat{q_{c}}\rangle_{\text{F}}+R\langle r_{c},\hat{r}_{c}\rangle_{\text{HF}}\,,

(D.81)

that for this ansatz we have for the rate function (
D.36
):

\displaystyle\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\Phi^{(R)}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)=-\gamma\rho_{a}\hat{\rho}_{a}+\frac{1}{p}\log\left(\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{0}}\left[\exp\left\{d\hat{\rho}_{a}\left\lVert\theta_{0}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}\right]\right)=-\gamma\left[\rho_{a}\hat{\rho}_{a}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(1-2\hat{\rho}_{a}\right)\right]

(D.82)

for any parameters
t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}
. As argued in Section
D.5
, we need the limit (
D.82
) to be
0
at the critical
t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R\downarrow 0)
and
\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R\downarrow 0)
for our ansatz to be consistent. Luckily, by setting the derivative of the right-hand side of (
D.82
) with respect to
\rho_{a}
to
0
, we see that

\hat{\rho}_{a}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right)=0
,
which results in
\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\Phi^{(R)}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(R)\right)=0
as desired. Furthermore, by setting the derivative of the right-hand side of (
D.82
) with respect to
\hat{\rho}_{a}
to
0
, we also obtain
\rho_{a}^{*}\left(R\downarrow 0\right)=1
,
as we should, since
\rho_{a}
corresponds to
\left\lVert\theta_{0}\right\rVert^{2}
which concentrates onto its expectation
1
in the proportional asymptotics limit.
As we assume with (
D.51
) that the critical points of
\Phi^{(R)}
converge to the critical point of
\Phi
, we will immediately use
\rho_{a}=1
and
\hat{\rho}_{a}=0
for all of the following calculations and optimality conditions for
\Phi
.

D.9
Calculating
\Psi_{y}

As discussed in Section
D.5
, we now want to obtain the
R
-derivative of the rate function (
D.36
) at
R=0
to subsequently calculate the optimality conditions for the evaluation of
\text{crit}_{t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}}\left(\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{R}\Phi^{(R)}\right)(t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}})

in (
D.51
). We start by considering the derivative of
\Psi_{y}^{(R)}
, as found in (
D.71
), in this section.
Using the identity
\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{R}\log\mathbb{E}\left[A(R)^{R}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\log A(0)\right]
to interchange logarithms and expectations in (
D.71
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Psi_{y}(t_{\mathrm{sym}}):=\frac{1}{\alpha}\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\Psi_{y}^{(R)}(t_{\mathrm{sym}})=-\frac{k+1}{2}\log(2\pi)-\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{2R}\log\det\left(\Sigma^{(1:R)}_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)+\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1}),\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\rho_{a})}\bigg[

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;P_{\text{data}}(\Upsilon\mid\omega,\varpi)\log\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Upsilon}\;L_{\beta}(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon})\exp\left\{\left\langle\tilde{\Upsilon}-\mu_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}},\sqrt{-\widetilde{q}}\;\xi-\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{r}-\widetilde{q}\right)\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}-\mu_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\right)\right\rangle\right\}\right)\bigg]

(D.83)

We complete the square in the innermost integral according to

\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle x,Ax\right\rangle+\left\langle J,x\right\rangle\right\}=\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle x-A^{-1}J,A\left(x-A^{-1}J\right)\right\rangle\right\}\exp\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left\langle J,A^{-1}J\right\rangle\right\},

(D.84)

with
x=\tilde{\Upsilon}-\mu_{\omega,t_{\mathrm{sym}}}
,
A=\widetilde{r}-\widetilde{q}
, and
J=\sqrt{-\widetilde{q}}\;\xi
. This gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Psi_{y}(t_{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\rho_{a})}\biggl[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times P(\Upsilon\mid\omega,\varpi)\log\left(\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\Upsilon}\sim\mathcal{N}(s+\rho_{a}^{-1}\omega f+(r-q)\sqrt{(r-q)^{-1}(q-\rho_{a}^{-1}f^{\otimes 2})(r-q)^{-1}}\xi,r-q)}[L_{\beta}(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon})]\right)\biggr].

(D.85)

By a change of variables in
\xi
and
\omega
we can re-write this expression as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha}\Psi_{y}(t_{\mathrm{sym}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\langle f,q^{-1/2}\xi\rangle,\;\rho_{a}-\langle f,q^{-1}f\rangle\right)}\left[P_{\text{data}}(\Upsilon\mid\omega,\varpi)\right]\log\left(\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\Upsilon}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(s+q^{1/2}\xi,\;r-q\right)}[L_{\beta}(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon})]\right)\right]

\displaystyle=:\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2})\right],

(D.86)

with means and covariances

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bar{m}_{1}=\left\langle f,q^{-1/2}\xi\right\rangle\in\mathbb{R}\\
\bar{m}_{2}=s+q^{1/2}\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1}\end{cases}\quad\begin{cases}\sigma_{1}^{2}=1-\left\langle f,q^{-1}f\right\rangle\in\mathbb{R}\\
\Sigma_{2}=r-q\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)\times(k+1)}\end{cases}

(D.87)

and the notation

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\left(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2}\right)&:=\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})}[P_{\text{data}}(\Upsilon\mid\omega,\varpi)]\\
\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\left(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2}\right)&:=\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\Upsilon}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2})}[L_{\beta}(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon})]\,,\end{cases}

(D.88)

analogous to
Gerace et al. 2021
. Altogether, our final result for the
R
-derivative of the potential
\Psi_{y}^{(R)}
reads

\displaystyle\Psi_{y}(t_{\mathrm{sym}})=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2})\right]\,.

(D.89)

D.10
Calculating
\Psi_{w}

Taking the limit
\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\Psi_{w}^{(R)}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)/R
is straightforward as the
R
dependence in (
D.80
) only manifests in the
R
-fold product over replicas. Using
\hat{\rho}_{a}=0
from the consistency check, we then end up with

\displaystyle\Psi_{w}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)

\displaystyle:=\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{R}\Psi_{w}^{(R)}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)

\displaystyle=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{\hat{q}_{b}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{a}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{c}}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,I_{p}\right)}\bigg[\log\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left(\frac{p}{2\pi}\right)^{p/2}\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{2}p\left\langle w,Aw\right\rangle+\sqrt{p}\left\langle w,J\right\rangle\right\}\mathrm{d}w\bigg)\bigg]\,.

(D.90)

with

\displaystyle A:=\beta\lambda I_{p}+A_{\hat{r}_{b}-\hat{q}_{b}}+A_{2\hat{r}_{a}-\hat{q}_{a}}+A_{2\hat{r}_{c}-\hat{q}_{c}}\,.

(D.91)

The Gaussian integral inside the logarithm evaluates to
\det^{-1/2}(A)\times\exp\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left\langle J,A^{-1}J\right\rangle\right\}
and using identity
\log\det=\trace\log
,
the potential in (
D.90
) becomes

\displaystyle\Psi_{w}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{\hat{q}_{b}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{a}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{c}}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,I_{p}\right)}\bigg[-\frac{1}{2}\trace\log A+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle J,A^{-1}J\right\rangle\bigg]\,.

(D.92)

For the quadratic form, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{\hat{q}_{b}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{a}},\eta_{\hat{q}_{c}}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,I_{p}\right)}\bigg[\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle J,A^{-1}J\right\rangle\bigg]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\trace\bigg[\big(J_{\hat{f}_{a}}J_{\hat{f}_{a}}^{\top}+2J_{\hat{f}_{a}}J_{\hat{f}_{b}}^{\top}+J_{\hat{f}_{b}}J_{\hat{f}_{b}}^{\top}+A_{\hat{q}_{a}}+A_{\hat{q}_{b}}+A_{\hat{q}_{c}}\big)A^{-1}\bigg]+\tfrac{1}{2}\left\langle J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}},A^{-1}(J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}})\right\rangle\,,

(D.93)

where

\displaystyle\begin{cases}J_{\hat{f}_{a}}J_{\hat{f}_{a}}^{\top}&=p(\hat{f}_{a})^{2}\kappa_{1}^{2}\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta\\
J_{\hat{f}_{a}}J_{\hat{f}_{b}}^{\top}&=p\hat{f}_{a}\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b})\\
J_{\hat{f}_{b}}J_{\hat{f}_{b}}^{\top}&=p(\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\textsc{diag}(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b})\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b})\\
&=p(\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b}\hat{f}_{b}^{\top}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta)\odot(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta)\,.\end{cases}

(D.94)

For convenience, we define

\displaystyle\Xi:=J_{\hat{f}_{a}}J_{\hat{f}_{a}}^{\top}+2J_{\hat{f}_{a}}J_{\hat{f}_{b}}^{\top}+J_{\hat{f}_{b}}J_{\hat{f}_{b}}^{\top}+A_{\hat{q}_{a}}+A_{\hat{q}_{b}}+A_{\hat{q}_{c}},

(D.95)

so that

\Psi_{w}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{2p}\bigg\{-\trace\log A+\trace(\Xi A^{-1})+\left\langle J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}},A^{-1}(J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}})\right\rangle\bigg\}.

(D.96)

D.11
Saddlepoint equations for
\Phi
at finite
\beta

To obtain first order optimality conditions, we will differentiate the
R
-derivative of (
D.36
) at
R=0
with respect to the overlap parameters as outlined in Section
D.5
.
Recalling from the consistency check of Section
D.8
that we can set
\rho_{a}=1
and
\hat{\rho}_{a}=0
, we have from (
D.81
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{R\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{R}\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\langle t,\hat{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=f_{a}\hat{f}_{a}+\left\langle f_{b},\hat{f}_{b}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}q_{a}\hat{q}_{a}+r_{a}\hat{r}_{a}-\left\langle q_{b},\hat{q}_{b}\right\rangle+\left\langle r_{b},\hat{r}_{b}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle q_{c},\hat{q}_{c}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}+\left\langle r_{c},\hat{r}_{c}\right\rangle_{\text{HF}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle f,\hat{f}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle q,\hat{q}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle r,\hat{r}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle r,\hat{r}\odot I_{k+1}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle f,\hat{f}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle q,\hat{q}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}+\left\langle r,\hat{r}\right\rangle_{\text{HF}}=\left\langle f,\hat{f}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\trace\left[q\hat{q}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\trace\left[r\hat{r}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\trace\left[r\left(\hat{r}\odot I_{k+1}\right)\right]\,.

(D.97)

Summarizing what we have obtained so far, we have now found the
R
-derivative of the rate function (
D.36
) for the replica-symmetric overlap parameter ansatz from Section
D.5
with

\displaystyle\Phi\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}\right|_{R=0}\Phi^{(R)}\right)\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{y}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)+\Psi_{y_{0}}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)+\Psi_{w}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)-\left(\left\langle f,\hat{f}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle q,\hat{q}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}+\left\langle r,\hat{r}\right\rangle_{\text{HF}}\right)\,,

(D.98)

where the potentials are given by

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Psi_{y}(t_{\mathrm{sym}})&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2})\;\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\right]\\[4.0pt]
\Psi_{y_{0}}(t_{\mathrm{sym}})&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})\log\mathcal{Z}[\tilde{L}_{\beta h}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2})\;\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\right]\\[4.0pt]
\Psi_{w}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{2p}\left\{-\trace\log A+\trace(\Xi A^{-1})+\left\langle J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}},A^{-1}(J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}})\right\rangle\right\}\end{cases}

(D.99)

and we refer to Sections
D.9
and
D.10
for further definitions and details.

As for the optimality conditions for
t_{\text{sym}}
and
\hat{t}_{\text{sym}}
, we note that because of the symmetry of
q_{c},r_{c},\hat{q}_{c},\hat{r}_{c}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, the lower and upper triangular elements are not independent. As detailed by
Srinivasan and Panda 2023
—and notably in contrast to
Petersen et al. 2008
and others—when differentiating a scalar function
f(S)
of a
symmetric
matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, the symmetric gradient
\nabla^{\text{sym}}
of
f
at
S
when varying the function on the manifold of symmetric
d\times d
matrices corresponds to

\displaystyle\nabla^{\text{sym}}f=\text{sym}\left(\nabla f\right)\,,

(D.100)

where the gradient on the right-hand side denotes differentiation of
f\colon\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\to\mathbb{R}
with respect to all
d^{2}
components individually, treating them as independent variables, and
\text{sym}(A)=\tfrac{1}{2}(A+A^{\top})
.

The result for the coupled system of optimality conditions for (
D.98
) at zero external field
h=0
, where
\Psi_{y_{0}}=0
, is then given by (using the aforementioned symmetric matrix derivative (
D.100
)):

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0\ \ \ =\nabla_{s}\Psi_{y}\,,\qquad\qquad&0\ \ \ =\nabla_{\hat{s}}\Psi_{w}\\
\hat{f}\ \ =\nabla_{f}\Psi_{y}\,,\qquad\qquad&f\ \ =\nabla_{\hat{f}}\Psi_{w}\\
\hat{q}=-2\,\text{sym}\left(\nabla_{q}\Psi_{y}\right)\,,\qquad\qquad&q=-2\,\text{sym}\left(\nabla_{\hat{q}}\Psi_{w}\right)\\
\hat{r}\odot\left(\1_{k+1}^{\otimes 2}+I_{k+1}\right)=2\,\text{sym}\left(\nabla_{r}\Psi_{y}\right)\,,\qquad\qquad&r\odot\left(\1_{k+1}^{\otimes 2}+I_{k+1}\right)=2\,\text{sym}\left(\nabla_{\hat{r}}\Psi_{w}\right)\end{cases}\,.

(D.101)

Here, we expect
\nabla_{\hat{s}}\Psi_{w}=0
to imply
\hat{s}=0
, and we also anticipate the implicit system of equations
\nabla_{s}\Psi_{y}=0
to admit a closed-form solution
s^{*}
for certain loss functions
\ell
in the low-temperature limit
\beta\to\infty
. We proceed with the evaluation of all derivatives now.

D.11.1
Derivatives of
\Psi_{y}

We recall that the integrand of the potential
\Psi_{y}
in (
D.89
) is

{\cal J}(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2},\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2})=\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})\cdot\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2}),

(D.102)

where

\bar{m}_{1}=\left\langle f,q^{-1/2}\xi\right\rangle\in\mathbb{R},\quad\sigma_{1}^{2}=1-\left\langle f,q^{-1}f\right\rangle>0,\quad\bar{m}_{2}=s+q^{1/2}\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1},\quad\Sigma_{2}=r-q\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)\times(k+1)}.

(D.103)

To aid in differentiation, we note that

\frac{\mathrm{d}{\cal J}}{\mathrm{d}*}=\frac{\partial{\cal J}}{\partial\bar{m}_{1}}\frac{\partial\bar{m}_{1}}{\partial*}+\frac{\partial{\cal J}}{\partial(\sigma_{1}^{2})}\frac{\partial(\sigma_{1}^{2})}{\partial*}+\sum_{i\in[k+1]}\frac{\partial{\cal J}}{\partial\langle e_{i},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle}\frac{\partial\langle e_{i},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle}{\partial*}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k+1]\,,\\
i\leq j\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathrm{d}{\cal J}}{\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{2,ij}}\frac{\partial\Sigma_{2,ij}}{\partial*}\,,

(D.104)

where
*
is a generic stand-in for an overlap parameter. In the above, we separate the partial derivatives the components of
\bar{m}_{2}
as we anticipate differentiating these quantities with respect to a matrix (otherwise, we would need to introduce cumbersome notation for third order tensors). Furthermore, as
\Sigma_{2}
is a covariance matrix in
{\cal J}
, it is necessarily symmetric and only has
(k+1)(k+2)/2
degrees of freedom for which we pick the upper right triangular part of
\Sigma_{2}
.

To find the four derivatives of
{\cal J}
, we apply Stein’s identity
Stein 1981
to obtain

\begin{cases}\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}{\cal J}=\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{(\sigma_{1}^{2})}{\cal J}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}^{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{\langle e_{i},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle}{\cal J}=\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\partial_{\langle e_{i},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\\[4.0pt]
\nabla_{\Sigma_{2}}{\cal J}=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\left(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}^{\otimes 2}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])^{\otimes 2}\right),\end{cases}

(D.105)

where for economy we have suppressed the arguments of the function.

We also find

\begin{cases}\nabla_{s}(\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2},\langle e_{i},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle,\Sigma_{2})=(0,\;0,\;e_{i},\;0)\\[4.0pt]
\nabla_{f}(\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2},\langle e_{i},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle,\Sigma_{2})=(q^{-1/2}\xi,\;-2q^{-1}f,\;0,\;0)\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{r}(\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2},\langle e_{k},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle,\Sigma_{2,ij})=(0,\;0,\;0,\;\frac{1}{2}(e_{i}\otimes e_{j}+e_{j}\otimes e_{i})),\end{cases}

(D.106)

and

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{q}\bar{m}_{1}&=-\frac{1}{2}\textsc{rs}[(q^{-1/2}\oplus q^{-1/2})^{-1}(q^{-1}\otimes q^{-1})\textsc{vec}\left(\xi\otimes f+f\otimes\xi\right)]\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{q}\sigma_{1}^{2}&=q^{-1}f^{\otimes 2}q^{-1}\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{q}\langle e_{k},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle&=\frac{1}{2}\textsc{rs}[(q^{1/2}\oplus q^{1/2})^{-1}\textsc{vec}\left(\xi\otimes e_{k}+e_{k}\otimes\xi\right)]\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{q}\Sigma_{2,ij}&=-\frac{1}{2}(e_{i}\otimes e_{j}+e_{j}\otimes e_{i}).\end{cases}

(D.107)

Then, we obtain from the optimality conditions (
D.101
) that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]]\\[6.0pt]
\hat{f}&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;q^{-1/2}\Big(\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\xi-\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}^{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]f\Big)]\\[6.0pt]
\hat{q}&=-2\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\biggl[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\textsc{rs}[(q^{-1/2}\oplus q^{-1/2})^{-1}(q^{-1}\otimes q^{-1})\textsc{vec}\left(\xi\otimes f+f\otimes\xi\right)]\;\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\\[6.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}q^{-1}f^{\otimes 2}q^{-1}\;(\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}^{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}])\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\\
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\textsc{rs}[(q^{1/2}\oplus q^{1/2})^{-1}\textsc{vec}(\xi\otimes\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\otimes\xi)]\\
&\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\left(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}^{\otimes 2}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])^{\otimes 2}\right)\Big)\biggr]\\[6.0pt]
\hat{r}\odot(\1_{k+1}^{\otimes 2}+I_{k+1})&=2\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\left(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}^{\otimes 2}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])^{\otimes 2}\right)\Big)\biggr]\end{cases}

(D.108)

The expression for
\hat{q}
can be simplified through applying the chain rule identities

\nabla_{\xi}=q^{-1/2}f\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}=q^{1/2}\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}.

(D.109)

By linearity of expectation, the
\xi
terms in the first components of
\hat{q}
and
\hat{f}
can be re-written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\xi\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\nabla_{\xi}(\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])]

(D.110)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:xichainrule}}{=}q^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[f\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}^{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]]+q^{1/2}\;\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]]\,.

(D.111)

Similarly, inspecting the
\xi
terms in the third component of
\hat{q}
, we note that after permutation we have quantities of the form

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\xi\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\partial_{\langle e_{k},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]]=\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\nabla_{\xi}(\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\partial_{\langle e_{k},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\left[q^{-1/2}f\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\partial_{\langle e_{k},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]q^{1/2}\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}(\partial_{\langle e_{k},\bar{m}_{2}\rangle}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])\right]\,.

(D.112)

Following this general strategy of converting multiplication by
\xi
into differentiation via Stein’s identity then applying the chain rule identities, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&=\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]]\\[6.0pt]
\hat{f}&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]]\\[6.0pt]
\hat{q}&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\biggl[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\textsc{rs}[(q^{-1/2}\oplus q^{-1/2})^{-1}(q^{-1}\otimes q^{-1})(q^{-1/2}\otimes I+I\otimes q^{-1})\textsc{vec}(f^{\otimes 2})]\;\partial^{2}_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\\[6.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\textsc{rs}[(q^{-1/2}\oplus q^{-1/2})^{-1}(q^{-1}\otimes q^{-1})\textsc{vec}\left(q^{1/2}\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\otimes f+f\otimes q^{1/2}\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\right)]\;\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\\[6.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}q^{-1}f^{\otimes 2}q^{-1}\;(\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}^{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}])\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\\[6.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\textsc{rs}[(q^{1/2}\oplus q^{1/2})^{-1}\textsc{vec}(q^{-1/2}f\otimes\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\otimes q^{-1/2}f)]\;\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\\[6.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\textsc{rs}[(q^{1/2}\oplus q^{1/2})^{-1}\textsc{vec}(q^{1/2}\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\otimes\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\otimes q^{1/2}\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])]\\[6.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\left(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}^{\otimes 2}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])^{\otimes 2}\right)\Big)\biggr]\\[6.0pt]
&\hskip-14.22636pt\hat{r}\odot(\1_{k+1}^{\otimes 2}+I_{k+1})=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\biggl[\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\left(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}^{\otimes 2}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]+(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])^{\otimes 2}\right)\Big)\biggr].\end{cases}

(D.113)

The expression for
\hat{q}
can be dramatically simplified: by making use of the identities

\left(q^{-1/2}\oplus q^{-1/2}\right)\left(q^{-1}\otimes q^{-1}\right)\left(q^{-1/2}\oplus q^{1/2}\right)=q^{-1}\otimes q^{-1}

(D.114)

and

\left(q^{1/2}\oplus q^{1/2}\right)^{-1}=\left(q^{-1/2}\otimes q^{-1/2}\right)^{-1}\left(q^{-1/2}\oplus q^{-1/2}\right)=\left(q^{-1/2}\oplus q^{-1/2}\right)\left(q^{-1/2}\otimes q^{-1/2}\right)^{-1},

(D.115)

which can be easily verified by
applying the eigenvalue decomposition for
q
, we obtain

\hat{q}=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\left[(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])^{\otimes 2}\right]\,.

(D.116)

It is also convenient to further define

\hat{\Sigma}_{2}:=\hat{q}-\hat{r}\odot\left(\1_{k+1}^{\otimes 2}+I_{k+1}\right)

(D.117)

so that the saddlepoint equations (
D.101
) from the
\Psi_{y}
-derivatives at finite
\beta
and for a generic loss function
\ell
finally reduce to

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]]\\[6.0pt]
\hat{f}&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\left[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;q^{-1/2}\Big(\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\xi-\partial_{\bar{m}_{1}}^{2}\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]f\Big)\right]\\[6.0pt]
\hat{q}&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\left[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot(\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}])^{\otimes 2}\right]\\[6.0pt]
\hat{\Sigma}_{2}&=-\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\biggl[\int\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}^{\otimes 2}\log\mathcal{Z}[L_{\beta}]\biggr]\,,\end{cases}

(D.118)

which should be compared to the corresponding expression for
L^{2}
training in
Gerace et al. 2021
.

D.11.2
Derivatives of
\Psi_{w}

For the
\hat{s}
derivatives of the potential
\Psi_{w}
as defined in (
D.96
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\nabla_{\hat{s}_{a}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\left(\kappa_{0}^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}A^{-1}\1_{p}\hat{s}_{a}+\kappa_{0}\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\1_{p}^{\top}A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{s}_{b}\right)\\[4.0pt]
\nabla_{\hat{s}_{b}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\left(\kappa_{0}\kappa_{0}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta A^{-1}\1_{p}\hat{s}_{a}+(\kappa_{0}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{s}_{b}\right)\,,\end{cases}

(D.119)

so at optimality by (
D.101
) we have

\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{0}^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}A^{-1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{0}\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\1_{p}^{\top}A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\\[6.0pt]
\kappa_{0}\kappa_{0}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta A^{-1}\1_{p}&(\kappa_{0}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{s}_{a}\\
\hat{s}_{b}\end{pmatrix}=0.

(D.120)

We expect this to imply
\hat{s}=0
at optimality.

For the
\hat{f}
derivatives of the potential
\Psi_{w}
as defined in (
D.96
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\nabla_{\hat{f}_{a}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\trace(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta A^{-1})\hat{f}_{a}+\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\trace(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta\textsc{diag}(\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b})A^{-1})\right)\\[4.0pt]
&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\trace(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta A^{-1})\hat{f}_{a}+\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\1_{p}^{\top}((A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta)\odot I_{p})\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b}\right)\\[4.0pt]
\nabla_{\hat{f}_{b}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\left(\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta((A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta)\odot I_{p})\1_{p}\hat{f}_{a}+(\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta(A^{-1}\odot(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta))\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\hat{f}_{b}\right)\,.\end{cases}

(D.121)

We shall also require the following lemma.

Lemma D.1
.

For all symmetric
A,B,C\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
we have the identity

\trace((A\odot B)C))=\trace((B\odot C)A)
.

Proof.

Consider the singular value decomposition of
B=\sum_{k}\sigma_{k}v_{k}v_{k}^{\top}
. Then

A\odot B=A\odot\left(\sum_{k}\sigma_{k}v_{k}v_{k}^{\top}\right)=\sum_{k}\sigma_{k}A\odot(v_{k}v_{k}^{\top})=\sum_{k}\sigma_{k}\textsc{diag}(v_{k})A\;\textsc{diag}(v_{k}).

(D.122)

Substituting this into the trace, we note that

\trace((A\odot B)C)=\sum_{k}\sigma_{k}\trace(\textsc{diag}(v_{k})A\;\textsc{diag}(v_{k})C)=\sum_{k}\sigma_{k}\trace(A\;\textsc{diag}(v_{k})C\;\textsc{diag}(v_{k}))=\trace(A(B\odot C)).

(D.123)

∎

This lemma shows, for example, the identity

\trace(A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}\Theta)=\trace(A^{-1}(\Theta^{\top}\Theta\odot\1_{p}\1_{p}^{\top}))=\trace((A^{-1}\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\1_{p}\1_{p}^{\top})=\1_{p}^{\top}(A^{-1}\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\1_{p}.

(D.124)

For
\hat{q}_{a},\hat{q}_{b},\hat{q}_{c}
the derivatives are

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\nabla_{\hat{q}_{a}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}-\frac{1}{2p}\trace\left(A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1}(\kappa_{1}^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+\kappa_{*}^{2}I_{p})\right)\\[4.0pt]
\nabla_{\hat{q}_{b}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}-\frac{1}{p}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\left((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1}\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}\Theta)\odot I_{p}\right)\1_{p}\\[4.0pt]
\nabla_{\hat{q}_{c}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}-\frac{1}{2p}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\left((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p})\right)\Theta^{\top}V_{k},\end{cases}

(D.125)

where for
\hat{q}_{c}
we made use of the lemma above to compute the symmetric gradient. More succinctly, making use of the identity
(\nabla_{Q}^{\text{sym}}f)_{ij}=(1-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{ij})\nabla_{q_{ij}}f
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\hat{q}}^{\text{sym}}\Psi_{w}=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{-1}{2p}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\;\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}

(D.126)

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{*}^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\1_{p}&0\\
0&\left(\kappa_{*}^{\prime}\right)^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\;\Bigg).

(D.127)

Similarly, for
\hat{r}_{a},\hat{r}_{b},\hat{r}_{c}
the derivatives are

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\nabla_{\hat{r}_{a}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\trace\left((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})(\kappa_{1}^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+\kappa_{*}^{2}I_{p})\right)\\[4.0pt]
\nabla_{\hat{r}_{b}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\left(((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\kappa_{1}\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}\Theta)\odot I_{p}\right)\1_{p}\\[4.0pt]
\nabla_{\hat{r}_{c}}\Psi_{w}&=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{2p}(V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\left((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot((\kappa_{1}^{\prime})^{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta+(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}I_{p})\right)\Theta^{\top}V_{k})\odot(I_{k}+\1_{k}\1_{k}^{\top}),\end{cases}

(D.128)

or altogether we can write

\displaystyle\nabla_{\hat{r}}^{\text{sym}}\Psi_{w}

\displaystyle=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{2p}\Bigg(\;\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}+\ldots

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}(\kappa_{*})^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\1_{p}&0\\
0&(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\;\Bigg)\odot(I_{k+1}+\1_{k+1}\1_{k+1}^{\top}).

(D.129)

To simplify the update for
f
further, we use the identity

\displaystyle\1^{\top}(AB\odot I)v=\trace(AB\;\textsc{diag}(v))=\trace(IB\;\textsc{diag}(v)A)=\1^{\top}(A\odot B)v

(D.130)

for symmetric
A
and generic
B
and
v
of appropriate dimensions.

All in all, from those optimality conditions in (
D.101
) that involve
\Psi_{w}
-derivatives, we obtain the set of equations

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\Bigg(\;\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{0}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{0}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}A^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{0}\1_{p}&\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\Bigg)\,\hat{s}\\[10.0pt]
f&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}(A^{-1}\odot(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\hat{f}\\[10.0pt]
q&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\Bigg(\;\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\\[1.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad+\begin{pmatrix}(\kappa_{*})^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\1_{p}&0\\
0&(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\;\Bigg)\\[4.0pt]
r&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\Bigg(\;\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\\[4.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad+\begin{pmatrix}(\kappa_{*})^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\1_{p}&0\\
0&(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta((A^{-1}+A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\;\Bigg)\,.\end{cases}

(D.131)

Using
\Sigma_{2}=r-q
instead of
r
, the last equation can be simplified to

\displaystyle\Sigma_{2}=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\;\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}(A^{-1}\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}

(D.132)

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}(\kappa_{*})^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}(A^{-1}\odot I_{p})\1_{p}&0\\
0&(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta(A^{-1}\odot I_{p})\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\;\Bigg)\,.

(D.133)

D.12
Training error as
\beta\to\infty
, and temperature scalings of the overlap parameters

Recall that the training error is given by (
D.9
) with the free energy density

\displaystyle f_{\beta}(h=0)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{crit}_{t_{\mathrm{sym}},\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}}\,\left\{\Psi_{y}(t_{\mathrm{sym}})+\Psi_{w}\left(\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}\right)-\left(\left\langle f,\hat{f}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle q,\hat{q}\right\rangle_{\text{F}}+\left\langle r,\hat{r}\right\rangle_{\text{HF}}\right)\right\}\,.

(D.134)

We hence would like to consider the low-temperature limit
\beta\to\infty
in the saddle-point equations derived so far. Making the
\beta
-dependence explicit, we can schematically write the free energy density as

\displaystyle f_{\beta}(0)=-\Phi_{\beta}\left(t^{*}_{\text{sym}}\left(\beta\right),\hat{t}^{*}_{\text{sym}}\left(\beta\right)\right)\quad\text{with}\quad\nabla_{t_{\mathrm{sym}}}\Phi_{\beta}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(\beta\right),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(\beta\right)\right)=\nabla_{\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}}\Phi_{\beta}\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(\beta\right),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(\beta\right)\right)=0\,,

(D.135)

with superscript
*
denoting the critical point. By using the chain rule and optimality conditions, similar to Section
D.5
, we have

\partial_{\beta}f_{\beta}(0)=-(\partial_{\beta}\Phi_{\beta})\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(\beta\right),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}\left(\beta\right)\right)\,,

meaning that we only have to explicitly differentiate
\Phi
in
\beta
and only need to insert the solution of the saddlepoint equations, without differentiating through them. Hence

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}f_{\beta}(0)=-\left(\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{w}\right)\left(\hat{t}^{*}_{\text{sym}}\right)-\left(\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{y}\right)\left(t^{*}_{\text{sym}}\right)\,.

(D.136)

Calculating these derivatives at finite
\beta
, then substituting the optimal overlap parameters and taking the limit
\beta\to\infty
, we find, since the only explicit
\beta
-dependence within
\Psi_{w}
as given by (
D.96
) is in
A
, that

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{w}=\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{-\lambda}{2p}\bigg(\trace\left[A^{-1}\right]+\trace\left[A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1}\right]+\left\langle J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}},A^{-2}(J_{\hat{s}_{a}}+J_{\hat{s}_{b}})\right\rangle\bigg)\,.

(D.137)

For the derivative
\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{y}
from (
D.89
), we note that

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{Z}\left[L_{\beta}\right]\left(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2}\right)=-\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\Upsilon}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(s+q^{1/2}\xi,r-q\right)}\left[\ell\left(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon}\right)\exp\left\{-\beta\ell\left(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon}\right)\right\}\right]\,,

(D.138)

such that

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{y}=-\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\left(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2}\right)\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Upsilon}\;\ell\left(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon}\right)\exp\left\{\frac{-1}{2}\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}-s+\sqrt{s}\xi\right)^{\top}(r-q)^{-1}\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}-s+\sqrt{s}\xi\right)-\beta\ell\left(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon}\right)\right\}}{(2\pi)^{(k+1)/2}\det(r-q)^{1/2}}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Upsilon}\;\exp\left\{\frac{-1}{2}\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}-s+\sqrt{s}\xi\right)^{\top}(r-q)^{-1}\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}-s+\sqrt{s}\xi\right)-\beta\ell\left(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon}\right)\right\}}{(2\pi)^{(k+1)/2}\det(r-q)^{1/2}}}\right]\,.

(D.139)

The form of (
D.139
) is essential in positing an ansatz for the critical overlap parameters

t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(\beta\to\infty)
and
\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(\beta\to\infty)
. We defer these computations to Subsection
D.12.1
below. This ansatz then permits us to obtain semi-analytical simplifications for the training error in the proportional asymptotics limit. Specifically,
using the scaling relations introduced in Subsection
D.12.1
below, in the low-temperature limit we have

r-q=\Sigma_{2}=\Sigma_{2}^{\infty}/\beta

whereas the other parameters in (
D.139
) do not scale with
\beta
. Applying Laplace’s method for the two
\tilde{\Upsilon}
integrals in the numerator and denominator of (
D.139
) as
\beta\to\infty
(while dropping the
\infty
superscripts) then leads to

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{y}=-\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}]\left(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2}\right)\cdot\ell\left(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*}\left(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2}\right)\right)\right]

(D.140)

where we defined the minimizer

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*}\left(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2}\right)=\argmin_{\tilde{\Upsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}-\bar{m}_{2}\right)^{\top}\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}-\bar{m}_{2}\right)+\ell(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon})\right]\,,

(D.141)

and the parameters are (all of which are
O(1)
in
\beta
)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{1}=\langle f,q^{-1/2}\xi\rangle\,,\quad\sigma_{1}^{2}=1-\langle f,q^{-1}f\rangle\,,\quad\bar{m}_{2}=s+q^{1/2}\xi\,,\quad\Sigma_{2}=r-q\,.

(D.142)

It is possible to further simplify the expression for the limit of
\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{y}
for specific loss functions
\ell
and data distributions
P_{\mathrm{data}}
. We detail these calculations for the Gaussian observation model and Sobolev training below in Section
D.14
.

D.12.1
Optimal overlap parameters as
\beta\to\infty

We will posit an ansatz for the optimal overlap parameters in the
\beta\to\infty
limit here. This reparameterization yields an effective low-temperature system of saddle point equations which only needs to be solved once, instead of for each element of an increasing sequence of
\beta
realizations. We will also consider the scaling of derived parameters

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Sigma_{2}&=r-q\\
\hat{\Sigma}_{2}&=\hat{q}-\hat{r}\odot\left(\1_{k+1}\1_{k+1}^{\top}+I_{k+1}\right)\end{cases}

(D.143)

To propose this ansatz, we first examine
\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{w}
as given in (
D.137
). The only explicit dependence of this expression on the inverse temperature
\beta
is through the matrix
A=\beta\lambda I_{p}+\dots
as defined through (
D.91
) and (
D.78
). Consequently, we expect that both
A
and
J
(the latter was defined in (
D.77
)) scale linearly with
\beta
. We can then expect

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\hat{s}=\beta\hat{s}^{\infty}\,,\qquad\quad&\hat{q}=\beta^{2}\hat{q}^{\infty}\,,\\
\hat{f}=\beta\hat{f}^{\infty}\,,\qquad\quad&\hat{\Sigma}_{2}=\beta\hat{\Sigma}_{2}^{\infty}\,.\end{cases}

(D.144)

We now consider
\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{y}
in (
D.139
). We will only obtain a nontrivial result, as calculated above using Laplace’s method in (
D.140
), if the integrals with respect to
\tilde{\Upsilon}
will contract about their value at (
D.141
). This behavior will occur only if
r-q
is of order
\beta^{-1}
while the other overlap parameters in (
D.139
) are constant in
\beta
. Thus, we define

\displaystyle\begin{cases}s=s^{\infty}\,,\qquad\quad&q=q^{\infty}\\
f=f^{\infty}\,,\qquad\quad&\Sigma_{2}=\frac{1}{\beta}\Sigma_{2}^{\infty}\end{cases}

(D.145)

Recalling the definitions

\displaystyle\begin{cases}A&=\beta\lambda I_{p}+\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\hat{\Sigma}_{2}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\[2.0pt]
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}\odot\Theta^{\top}\Theta+\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{*}\1_{p}&\kappa_{*}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\Sigma}_{2,a}&0\\
0&\hat{\Sigma}_{2,c}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{*}\1_{p}^{\top}\\[2.0pt]
\kappa_{*}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}\odot I_{p}\\
\Xi&=p\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\left(\hat{f}\hat{f}^{\top}\right)\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\[2.0pt]
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}\odot\left(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta\right)+\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\hat{q}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\[2.0pt]
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}\odot\Theta^{\top}\Theta\\
&\qquad\qquad+\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{*}\1_{p}&\kappa_{*}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{q}_{a}&0\\
0&\hat{q}_{c}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{*}\1_{p}^{\top}\\[2.0pt]
\kappa_{*}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}\odot I_{p}\end{cases}

from (
D.91
), (
D.95
), (
D.78
) and (
D.77
), we can further define
A=\beta A^{\infty}
and
\Xi=\beta^{2}\Xi^{\infty}
.
In particular, we then see from (
D.137
) that

\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{w}=\plim_{p\to\infty}\tfrac{-\lambda}{2p}\trace\left[A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1}\right]

in terms of the zero-temperature parameters as long as
\hat{s}=0
, which leads to (
2.26
) for the regularization term at optimality in the main text.

We now use the zero-temperature parameters to construct a set of corresponding saddle point equations. All overlap parameters in the following are also in the
\beta\to\infty
regime but we suppress the
\infty
superscripts for concision. We can then write from (
D.131
) and (
D.133
) that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}f&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}(A^{-1}\odot(\Theta^{\top}\theta_{0}\theta_{0}^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\hat{f}\\[10.0pt]
q&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\Bigg(\ \begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\\[10.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\begin{pmatrix}(\kappa_{*})^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\1_{p}&0\\
0&(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta((A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1})\odot I_{p})\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\ \Bigg)\\[10.0pt]
\Sigma_{2}&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\Bigg(\ \begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}^{\top}\\
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta\end{pmatrix}(A^{-1}\odot(\Theta^{\top}\Theta))\begin{pmatrix}\kappa_{1}\1_{p}&\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\\[10.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\begin{pmatrix}(\kappa_{*})^{2}\1_{p}^{\top}(A^{-1}\odot I_{p})\1_{p}&0\\
0&(\kappa_{*}^{\prime})^{2}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta(A^{-1}\odot I_{p})\Theta^{\top}V_{k}\end{pmatrix}\ \Bigg).\end{cases}

(D.146)

With this, we have derived (
2.22
) in the main text. Notably, this set of equations does not depend on the choice
\ell
. Similarly, we use the fact that
{\cal N}(\bar{m}_{1},\Sigma_{2})
concentrates in the low temperature limit to find from (
D.118
) that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\omega,\Upsilon}[\Sigma_{2}^{-1}(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*}-\bar{m}_{2})]\\[4.0pt]
\hat{f}&=\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\omega,\Upsilon}\left[\frac{\omega-\bar{m}_{1}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\Sigma_{2}^{-1}(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*}-\bar{m}_{2})\right]=\alpha\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\omega}\left[\partial_{\omega}\mathbb{E}_{\Upsilon\mid\omega}[\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*}]\right]\\[4.0pt]
\hat{q}&=\alpha\;\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\omega,\Upsilon}[(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*}-\bar{m}_{2})^{\otimes 2}]\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\\[4.0pt]
\hat{\Sigma}_{2}&=\alpha\Sigma_{2}^{-1}(I_{k+1}-\mathbb{E}_{\xi,\omega,\Upsilon}\left[\nabla_{\bar{m}_{2}}\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*\top}\right]).\end{cases}

(D.147)

where
\xi\sim{\cal N}(0,I_{k+1})
and
\omega\sim{\cal N}(\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})
. The second equality for
\hat{f}
is obtained by recognizing
\omega
is conditionally independent of
\bar{m}_{2}
given
\xi
and applying Stein’s identity. Note that the first condition provides an implicit optimality condition for
s
. Here, it now only remains to specify
\ell
to the standard subspace Sobolev loss (
D.2
) in order to arrive at (
2.21
) from the main text.

D.13
Calculating the generalization error

We can evaluate the generalization error (
D.3
) from (
D.10
) and (
D.98
) via

\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\varepsilon_{\text{gen}}(w^{*})\mid\varpi=-\frac{1}{\alpha}\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\frac{1}{\beta}\left(\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial h}\right|_{h=0}\Psi_{y_{0}}\right)\left(t_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(h=0),\hat{t}_{\mathrm{sym}}^{*}(h=0)\right)\,,

(D.148)

which requires, by the same reasoning as in previous Sections
D.5
and
D.12
, only the partial derivative of the rate function
\Phi
in
h
. Starting from the expression for the potential
\Psi_{y_{0}}
given in (
D.99
), we differentiate in
h
at
h=0
to obtain

\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\varepsilon_{\text{gen}}(w^{*})\mid\varpi=\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim{\cal N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{k+1}}\mathrm{d}\Upsilon\;\mathcal{Z}[P_{\text{data}}](\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})\;\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\Upsilon}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2}/\beta)}\left[\left\lVert\Upsilon-\tilde{\Upsilon}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim{\cal N}(0,I_{k+1})}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim{\cal N}(\langle f,q^{-1/2}f\rangle,1-\langle f,q^{-1}f\rangle)}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\Upsilon\sim P_{\text{data}}(\cdot\mid\omega,\varpi)}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\Upsilon}\sim{\cal N}(s+q^{1/2}\xi,\Sigma_{2}/\beta)}\left[\left\lVert\Upsilon-\tilde{\Upsilon}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\right]\right]\right]

(D.149)

where we have already made the
\beta
-scaling of all quantities explicit. By calculating the mean and covariance of the jointly normal random variables
(\xi,\omega,\tilde{\Upsilon})
in this expression, we find that as
\beta\to\infty
, we can write (
D.149
) as

\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\varepsilon_{\text{gen}}(w^{*})\mid\varpi=\mathbb{E}_{(\omega,\tilde{\Upsilon})}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\Upsilon\sim P_{\text{data}}(\cdot\mid\omega,\varpi)}\left[\left\lVert\Upsilon-\tilde{\Upsilon}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\right]\quad\text{ with }\quad(\omega,\tilde{\Upsilon})^{\top}\sim{\cal N}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
s\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&f^{\top}\\
f&q\end{pmatrix}\right)\,.

(D.150)

Of course, this corresponds to the definition of the
H^{1}_{k}
generalization error (
D.3
) where we have effectively simply replaced the network output using the Gaussian equivalence theorem, as discussed around (
2.16
) in the main text already. While we could have arrived at this conclusion immediately on an intuitive level, as we did in the main text, the systematic derivation of the generalization error via an external field
h
in the partition function makes it clear why exactly the overlap parameters as determined from the replica-symmetric saddle-point equations are indeed related to the generalization error. Finally, for the specific case of an additive Gaussian observation model (
2.5
), it is then straightforward to see that (
D.150
) implies (
2.17
) and (
2.18
) in the main text.

D.14
Specifying the setup to standard subspace Sobolev loss and additive Gaussian noise observations

Here, we want to simplify the saddle-point equations (
D.147
) and the training error term (
D.140
) as much as possible for the standard loss function (
D.2
) given by
\ell(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon})=\frac{1}{2}\|\Upsilon-\tilde{\Upsilon}\|^{2}
. The minimizer in (
D.141
) becomes

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}^{*}_{\ell}(\Upsilon;\bar{m}_{2},\Sigma_{2})\ =\ (\Sigma_{2}^{-1}+I_{k+1})^{-1}\left(\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\bar{m}_{2}+\Upsilon\right).

(D.151)

If we further assume the Gaussian observation model (
2.5
)
we compute the necessary quantities in (
D.147
) as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}_{\Upsilon\mid\omega}\left[\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\Upsilon}\|\Upsilon-\tilde{\Upsilon}^{*}_{\ell}\|^{2}\right]=\left(\Sigma_{2}+I_{k+1}\right)^{-2}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\phi(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}-\bar{m}_{2}\right)\\[6.0pt]
\mathbb{E}_{\Upsilon\mid\omega}\left[\Sigma_{2}^{-1}(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*}-\bar{m}_{2})\right]=(\Sigma_{2}+I_{k+1})^{-1}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\phi(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}-\bar{m}_{2}\right)\\[6.0pt]
\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\Upsilon\mid\omega}\left[(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\ell}^{*}-\bar{m}_{2})^{\otimes 2}\right]\Sigma_{2}^{-1}=(\Sigma_{2}+I_{k+1})^{-1}\left(C_{\eta}+\left(\begin{pmatrix}\phi(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}-\bar{m}_{2}\right)^{\otimes 2}\right)(\Sigma_{2}+I_{k+1})^{-1}\end{cases}

(D.152)

Putting everything together, we can then simplify (
D.147
) to

\displaystyle\begin{cases}s_{a}&=\mathbb{E}[\phi(\omega)]\1_{\kappa_{0}\neq 0}\\
s_{b}&=\varpi\mathbb{E}[\phi^{\prime}(\omega)]\1_{\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\neq 0}\\
\hat{f}&=\alpha(\Sigma_{2}+I_{k+1})^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}[\phi^{\prime}]\\
\varpi\mathbb{E}[\phi^{\prime\prime}]\end{pmatrix}\\[10.0pt]
\hat{q}&=\alpha(\Sigma_{2}+I_{k+1})^{-1}\Bigg(C_{\eta}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\begin{pmatrix}\phi(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}-\bar{m}_{2}\right)^{\otimes 2}\right]\Bigg)(\Sigma_{2}+I_{k+1})^{-1}\\
\hat{\Sigma}_{2}&=\alpha\left(\Sigma_{2}^{-1}-\Sigma_{2}^{-1}(\Sigma_{2}^{-1}+I_{k+1})^{-1}\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\right)=\alpha(I_{k+1}+\Sigma_{2})^{-1}\end{cases}

(D.153)

Note that the expectations in (
D.153
) can all be reduced to one-dimensional Gaussian integrals with respect to
\omega\sim{\cal N}(0,1)
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{k+1})}\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim{\cal N}(\langle f,q^{-1/2}\xi\rangle,1-\langle f,q^{-1}f\rangle)}\left[\left(\begin{pmatrix}\phi(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}-s-q^{1/2}\xi\right)^{\otimes 2}\right]

\displaystyle=q+\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}\left[\left(\begin{pmatrix}\phi(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}-s\right)^{\otimes 2}\right]-f\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\omega\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}\right]\otimes f\,,

(D.154)

which finally leads us to (
2.21
) in the main text (note that we kept a general weight
\tau>0
instead of
\tau=1
for the derivative term of the loss function in the main text, but the corresponding saddlepoint equations for general
\tau
can be derived from straightforward modifications of the calculations presented in this section). As for the training error (
D.134
), for the loss function
\ell(\Upsilon,\tilde{\Upsilon})=\frac{1}{2}\|\Upsilon-\tilde{\Upsilon}\|^{2}
, the expression (
D.140
) becomes

\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\partial_{\beta}\Psi_{y}=-\frac{\alpha}{2}\trace\Bigg((\Sigma_{2}+I_{k+1})^{-2}\left(C_{\eta}+\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim{\cal N}(0,I_{k+1})}\mathbb{E}_{\omega\mid\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(\bar{m}_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2})}\bigg[\left(\begin{pmatrix}\phi(\omega)\\
\varpi\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\end{pmatrix}-\bar{m}_{2}\right)^{\otimes 2}\bigg]\right)\Bigg)=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\hat{q}_{a}+\trace[\hat{q}_{c}]\right)

(D.155)

using (
D.153
), which hence leads us to (
2.24
) and (
2.25
) in the main text for the training error at optimality. To recognize that
\hat{q}_{a}
indeed corresponds to the
L^{2}
part of the training error and
\trace[\hat{q}_{c}]
to the
H^{1}_{k}
semi-norm part, as claimed in the main text, we could have perturbed
L_{\beta}
throughout all derivations of this section as

L_{\beta}(h_{1},h_{2}):=\exp\Bigg\{-\beta(\Upsilon-\tilde{\Upsilon})^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}(1+h_{1})&0^{\top}\\
0&(1+h_{2})I_{k}\end{pmatrix}(\Upsilon-\tilde{\Upsilon})\Bigg\}

(D.156)

and differentiate with respect to either
h_{1}
or
h_{2}
at
0
to isolate the respective part of the training error. The result is the identification (
2.24
) and (
2.25
) as expected.

Appendix E
Simplifications in the
L^{2}
training setting

This appendix contains a number of technical details for the simplifications of the replica-symmetric saddlepoint equations to the case of
L^{2}
training without gradients discussed in Remark
2.3
. This setting reduces to
Gerace et al. 2021
;
Goldt et al. 2022
, except that we also compute the
H^{1}_{k}
generalization error produced by training with
L^{2}
loss.

E.1
The distribution of
(\varpi,s_{b})
for
L^{2}
training

In this subsection, we motivate (
2.29
) for the joint distribution of the alignment parameter
\varpi=V_{k}^{\top}\theta_{0}
and the projected network gradient mean
s_{b}=\kappa_{0}^{\prime}V_{k}^{\top}\Theta w^{*}
for
L^{2}
training. Notably, equation (
2.29
) departs from (
2.19
) where
\varpi
and
s_{b}=\varpi\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\right]\1_{\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\neq 0}
are perfectly correlated for any
\tau>0
. The key difference between these two situations is that
w^{*}
is independent of
V_{k}
for
\tau=0
only, and there is otherwise some additional randomness in
s_{b}
that is independent of
\varpi
. Let us assume based on the numerical evidence in Figure
11
that
(\varpi,s_{b})
for
\tau=0
are jointly normally distributed in the proportional asymptotics limit with a non-degenerate covariance matrix. Of course, their mean will be
\mathbb{E}\left[(\varpi,s_{b})\right]=0
by independence of
V_{k}
from all other random quantities for
L^{2}
training. It remains to evaluate their second moments:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}&\mathbb{E}\left[\varpi_{i}\varpi_{j}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle v_{i},\theta_{0}\right\rangle\left\langle v_{j},\theta_{0}\right\rangle\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\theta_{0}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\delta_{ij}=\delta_{ij}\,,\\
&\mathbb{E}\left[\varpi_{i}s_{b,j}\right]=\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle v_{i},\theta_{0}\right\rangle\left\langle v_{j},\Theta w^{*}\right\rangle\right]=\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\theta_{0},\Theta w^{*}\right\rangle\right]\delta_{ij}\overset{\eqref{eq:overlap-def}}{=}f_{a}\delta_{ij}\,,\\
&\mathbb{E}\left[s_{b,i}s_{b,j}\right]=\left(\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle v_{i},\Theta w^{*}\right\rangle\left\langle v_{j},\Theta w^{*}\right\rangle\right]=\left(\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Theta w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\delta_{ij}\overset{\eqref{eq:overlap-def}}{=}\left(q_{a}-\kappa_{*}^{2}\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right)\delta_{ij}\,.\end{cases}

(E.1)

These results lead us to (
2.29
) in the main text. Conditioned on the alignment parameter, the distribution of the overlap parameter
s_{b}\mid\varpi
becomes Gaussian with mean
f_{a}\varpi
and variance
(q_{a}-\kappa_{*}^{2}\|w^{*}\|^{2}-f_{a}^{2})I_{k}
.

(a)

\tau=0
(
L^{2}
training)

(b)

\tau=1
(standard Sobolev training)

Figure 11
:
Joint distribution of
(\varpi,s_{b})
for
k=1
, comparing
L^{2}
training (
\tau=0
, left) and Sobolev training (
\tau=1
, right) from samples. The histograms are generated from
5000
samples of
\varpi=\left\langle v,\theta_{0}\right\rangle
and
s_{b}=\kappa_{0}^{\prime}\left\langle v,\Theta w^{*}\right\rangle
at
\alpha=1.25
,
\gamma=0.75
,
\lambda=1.25\cdot 10^{-4}
in dimension
d=1600
, with
\sigma=\text{SiLU}
,
\phi(\omega)=\omega+1/\cosh\omega
, iid Gaussian random features, and noiseless data
C_{\eta}=0
. The dashed lines are the theoretical predictions for the PDFs from solving the replica-symmetric saddlepoint equations: marginally
\varpi\sim{\cal N}(0,1)
in both cases, but
s_{b}=\varpi\mathbb{E}\left[\phi^{\prime}(\omega)\right]=\varpi
for
\tau>0
is expected to be perfectly correlated while for
\tau=0
the parameters
(\varpi,s_{b})
are jointly centered and nondegenerate normally distributed with
\mathbb{E}\left[\varpi s_{b}\right]=f_{a}=0.7102
and
\mathbb{E}\left[s_{b}^{2}\right]=q_{a}-\kappa_{*}^{2}\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}=1.2683
.

E.2
Expressing the random matrix traces for
L^{2}
training as Stieltjes transforms

Here, we want to simplify the saddlepoint equations for
\Sigma_{a},f_{a},q_{a}
in (
2.22
) for
L^{2}
training where (
2.28
) holds. We obtain from (
2.22
) that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Sigma_{a}&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\trace\left[A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\right]+\kappa_{*}^{2}\trace\left[A^{-1}\right]\right)\\[6.0pt]
f_{a}&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\kappa_{1}^{2}\trace\left[A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\right]\hat{f}_{a}\\[6.0pt]
q_{a}&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}\trace\left[A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\right]+\kappa_{*}^{2}\trace\left[A^{-1}\Xi A^{-1}\right]\right)\\[6.0pt]
&=\displaystyle\plim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1}{p}\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}\left(\hat{q}_{a}+\hat{f}_{a}^{2}/\gamma\right)\trace\left[\left(\Theta A^{-1}\Theta^{\top}\right)^{2}\right]+\kappa_{*}^{4}\hat{q}_{a}\trace\left[A^{-2}\right]+\kappa_{1}^{2}\kappa_{*}^{2}\left(2\hat{q}_{a}+\hat{f}_{a}^{2}/\gamma\right)\trace\left[\Theta A^{-2}\Theta^{\top}\right]\right)\end{cases}

(E.2)

where
A
and
\Xi
are given by (
2.31
). Writing
A=c_{2}\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}I_{p}+\Theta^{\top}\Theta\right)
with
c_{2}=\kappa_{1}^{2}\hat{\Sigma}_{a}
and defining

z:=\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}=\frac{\lambda+\kappa_{*}^{2}\hat{\Sigma}_{a}}{\kappa_{1}^{2}\hat{\Sigma}_{a}},

(E.3)

for all
k\in\mathbb{R}
we have the following useful identities:

\displaystyle\trace\left[\left(c_{1}I_{p}+c_{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\right)^{k}\right]

\displaystyle=c_{2}^{k}\left(z^{k}(p-d)+\trace\left[\left(zI_{d}+\Theta\Theta^{\top}\right)^{k}\right]\right)\;,

(E.4)

\displaystyle\Theta\left(c_{1}I_{p}+c_{2}\Theta^{\top}\Theta\right)^{k}\Theta^{\top}
